# Proposed OEIS comments: A232155 to A234407

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A232155

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A232155>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [7092](../papers/7001-7500/7092-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) + 2*a(n-2) - 13*a(n-3) - 5*a(n-4) + 14*a(n-5) - 3*a(n-6) + 3*a(n-7) + 8*a(n-8) + a(n-9) - a(n-10) for n>11.
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(1 + 5*x - 7*x^2 - 20*x^3 - 3*x^4 + 24*x^5 + 5*x^6 + 7*x^7 + 15*x^8 + 2*x^9 - 2*x^10) / (1 - 4*x - 2*x^2 + 13*x^3 + 5*x^4 - 14*x^5 + 3*x^6 - 3*x^7 - 8*x^8 - x^9 + x^10). - _Colin Barker_, Oct 03 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 89 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 11. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [6317](../papers/6001-6500/6317-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 17*a(n-1) +66*a(n-2) +338*a(n-3) +35*a(n-4) +706*a(n-5) -4131*a(n-6) +6628*a(n-7) -20192*a(n-8) +4632*a(n-9) -22908*a(n-10) +92304*a(n-11) -22608*a(n-12) -9536*a(n-13) -7616*a(n-14) +4096*a(n-15)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 757 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 14. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [7561](../papers/7501-8000/7561-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) +a(n-2) +13*a(n-3) +26*a(n-5) -4*a(n-6) +24*a(n-7) +16*a(n-8) for n>9.
> Empirical g.f.: x + 2*x^2*(-7 -16*x -30*x^2 +7*x^3 -17*x^4 -30*x^5 -4*x^6 +8*x^7) / ( (2*x^2+1)*(8*x^6 +12*x^5 -6*x^4 +7*x^3 +3*x^2 +3*x-1) ). - _R. J. Mathar_, Nov 27 2013

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 77 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 9. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Paper: [316](../papers/0001-0500/0316-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrences in the linked file are correct. Column k counts arrays of a fixed width, so it is a walk count in a finite graph and satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one, its order is the order stated for that column, and so is the order of every tail, so any recurrence of that order is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A232582

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A232582>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3205](../papers/3001-3500/3205-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) - a(n-2) + a(n-3) = 2*A005314(n-1).
> Empirical: G.f.: -2*x^2 / ( -1+2*x-x^2+x^3 ). - _R. J. Mathar_, Nov 23 2014

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 90 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [9238](../papers/9001-9500/9238-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 31*a(n-1) - 35*a(n-2) + 5*a(n-3).
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Oct 06 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence follows from the generating function this entry already states as fact: a recurrence with constant coefficients is exactly a denominator of the generating function, so the two are the same statement, and it holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```

## A233085

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A233085>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2742](../papers/2501-3000/2742-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 49*a(n-1) -450*a(n-2) +1466*a(n-3) -1853*a(n-4) +789*a(n-5) for n>8

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. The array grows sideways, so it has to be counted a column at a time, but the clause about which value turns up first is stated in row major order, and a walk across columns does not read the cells in that order. What the walk can carry is, for each named value, the lowest row it has turned up in so far, together with the order those lowest rows were reached; the clause itself is checked only at the end, since a value's lowest row can still drop later. That makes a(n) a walk count on 199 states, so it satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence and checking the one above is a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A233101

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A233101>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [1981](../papers/1501-2000/1981-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 25*a(n-1) +52*a(n-2) -1084*a(n-3) +16*a(n-4) +9754*a(n-5) -16165*a(n-6) +5229*a(n-7) +5498*a(n-8) -3740*a(n-9) +320*a(n-10) +96*a(n-11) for n>12

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. The array grows sideways, so it has to be counted a column at a time, but the clause about which value turns up first is stated in row major order, and a walk across columns does not read the cells in that order. What the walk can carry is, for each named value, the lowest row it has turned up in so far, together with the order those lowest rows were reached; the clause itself is checked only at the end, since a value's lowest row can still drop later. That makes a(n) a walk count on 163 states, so it satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence and checking the one above is a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 12. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A233219

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A233219>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2728](../papers/2501-3000/2728-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 111*a(n-1) -2128*a(n-2) +10532*a(n-3) -17559*a(n-4) +9045*a(n-5) for n>7

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. The array grows sideways, so it has to be counted a column at a time, but the clause about which value turns up first is stated in row major order, and a walk across columns does not read the cells in that order. What the walk can carry is, for each named value, the lowest row it has turned up in so far, together with the order those lowest rows were reached; the clause itself is checked only at the end, since a value's lowest row can still drop later. That makes a(n) a walk count on 324 states, so it satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence and checking the one above is a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 7. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A233240

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A233240>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2801](../papers/2501-3000/2801-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 23*a(n-1) - 81*a(n-2) - 143*a(n-3) + 82*a(n-4) + 120*a(n-5) for n>6.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(3 - 50*x + 77*x^2 + 239*x^3 + 31*x^4 - 75*x^5) / ((1 - x)*(1 + x)*(1 - 6*x)*(1 - 17*x - 20*x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Oct 10 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This is true. The array grows sideways, so it has to be counted a column at a time, but the clause about which value turns up first is stated in row major order, and a walk across columns does not read the cells in that order. What the walk can carry is, for each named value, the lowest row it has turned up in so far, together with the order those lowest rows were reached; the clause itself is checked only at the end, since a value's lowest row can still drop later. That makes a(n) a walk count on 37 states, so it satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence and checking the one above is a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A233630

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A233630>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [235](../papers/0001-0500/0235-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 63 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 256 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 63, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrences in the linked file are correct. Column k counts arrays of a fixed width, so it is a walk count in a finite graph and satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one, its order is the order stated for that column, and so is the order of every tail, so any recurrence of that order is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A234078

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A234078>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [140](../papers/0001-0500/0140-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 97 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 625 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 97, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrences in the linked file are correct. ```

## A234269

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A234269>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [9478](../papers/9001-9500/9478-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture D-finite with recurrence: -(n+1)*(2*n-5)*a(n) -2*n*(n-4)*a(n-1) +4*(n-1)*(2*n-3)*a(n-2) +2*(8*n^2-32*n+29)*a(n-3) +4*(n-2)*(2*n-7)*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jan 24 2020

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 3, so the recurrence holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A234401

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A234401>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [244](../papers/0001-0500/0244-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 60 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 343 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 60, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
